╭─ ⚕ Hermes ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
    Yes—that is the correct architectural frame.
    
    This is not “a 2D Dragon plus a 3D Dragon.” It is:
    
    text
    ONE DRAGON
    ├── one identity
    ├── one persisted conversation
    ├── one perception pipeline
    ├── one safety boundary
    ├── one request/response channel
    └── two embodiments
        ├── World embodiment: flying 3D Dragon
        └── HUD embodiment: smaller 2D Dragon
    
    
    The transformation should change presentation and control context, not the agent.
    
    What must remain identical in both perspectives
    
    The proven 2D Dragon already has:
    
    - manual player input
    - spoken narrative responses
    - persisted Hermes identity and context
    - request and capture correlation
    - pre-dispatch viewport capture
    - hash-verified image grounding
    - stale-response rejection
    - a 180-second response window
    - observation-only response enforcement
    - one active request at a time
    
    The 3D Dragon currently has only:
    
    - world-space flight/orbit animation
    - a HUD text input
    - a temporary runtime session ID
    - a simple HTTP bridge to the absent :8081 parser
    - Dragon/Lore text display
    - a response pulse effect
    
    So we should preserve the 3D presentation but replace its incomplete agent plumbing with the proven Dragon capability
    boundary.
    
    The key design rule
    
    The bridge must not live inside either visual representation.
    
    Conceptually:
    
    text
    DragonAgentCore
    ├── persisted Hermes identity
    ├── input submission
    ├── perception capture
    ├── request correlation
    ├── response validation
    ├── speech events
    └── embodiment state
    
    DragonWorld3D
    ├── flight
    ├── movement
    ├── world camera relationship
    └── visual reaction to DragonAgentCore speech
    
    DragonHUD2D
    ├── compact HUD position
    ├── text-entry interface
    ├── speech presentation
    └── visual reaction to DragonAgentCore speech
    
    
    Then transformation is approximately:
    
    text
    WORLD_3D
    → preserve the same DragonAgentCore
    → hide/transition the world representation
    → activate the small HUD representation
    → HUD_2D
    
    
    No new session. No second model. No duplicated memory. No handoff between agents.
    
    Vision also follows the perspective
    
    The capture should remain tied to the player’s current visible viewport:
    
    - In 3D world mode, the Dragon sees the world camera’s player view.
    - In HUD mode, the Dragon sees the same current player viewport, including the HUD where appropriate.
    - The representation that happens to be visible does not own perception.
    - The shared Dragon core owns request-correlated capture.
    
    The old 3D project is valuable because it already proves the missing presentation:
    
    - 3D world placement
    - orbital flight and bobbing
    - billboarded animated Dragon art
    - bridge-driven visual response pulse
    - a HUD communication surface
    
    And the copied GodotOllama dock is separate. It is an editor tool, not the Dragon’s mind.
    
    The major decision before any edits is which repository becomes the single canonical project. My engineering
    recommendation is to preserve the proven 2D/Hermes core and add the 3D embodiment around it, rather than transplanting
    the proven capability into an unversioned prototype—but the owning repository must be your decision.

    Understood. That corrects two important assumptions.
    
    Repository check
    
    https://github.com/SmokesBowls/godot_3d_avatar is reachable and currently appears empty:
    
    - git ls-remote succeeded
    - no HEAD or branch refs were returned
    - the local folder remains untouched and is still not initialized as a Git repository
    
    I will not initialize, connect, commit, or push it until you say the repository setup is ready.
    
    Correct architecture
    
    GodotOllama is:
    
    text
    portable editor tool
    ≠ Dragon brain
    ≠ project-specific runtime
    
    
    The byte-identical copy in the 3D project already proves that portability. When it is converted later, portability
    remains a requirement: it should continue to drop into different Godot projects without inheriting a project’s Dragon
    identity, runtime state, or canon.
    
    The immediate 3D goal is narrower:
    
    text
    Proven 2D Dragon capabilities
    → reproduced for the 3D Dragon
    → same Hermes session ID
    → same snapshot-based perception
    → same read-only safety boundary
    
    
    Snapshot correction
    
    You are right: the current 2D proof does not bind the Dragon semantically to a particular camera.
    
    It captures the visible viewport as an image at request time:
    
    text
    human submits message
    → generate client_request_id
    → capture current viewport snapshot
    → hash and correlate snapshot
    → pass image to the same Hermes session
    → validate correlated observational response
    
    
    For initial 3D parity, we should do exactly that—capture the current viewport snapshot. We do not need to introduce:
    
    - Dragon-eye cameras
    - player-camera ownership
    - camera-selection policy
    - multiple viewports
    - line-of-sight
    - frustum semantics
    - world-space perception
    - continuous vision
    
    Those are later perception features. They are not prerequisites for capability parity.
    
    First implementation boundary after the repository is ready
    
    The first work should be staged as:
    
    1. Baseline preservation
       - Initialize the existing 3D folder as the new repository.
       - Connect it to SmokesBowls/godot_3d_avatar.
       - Commit the current untouched prototype before changing behavior.
       - Preserve the portable Ollama addon exactly.
    
    2. Dragon identity parity
       - Use persisted Hermes session ID 20260731_065008_63a62d.
       - Replace the temporary startup-generated S_<timestamp>_<random> identity.
       - Reject session substitution.
    
    3. Snapshot parity
       - Capture one viewport PNG per submitted message.
       - Correlate it to a new client_request_id and capture_id.
       - Preserve the existing pre-dispatch capture phase.
       - Verify paths and hashes before attachment.
       - Pass the validated absolute image path through Hermes --image.
    
    4. Request/response parity
       - Use the same mailbox protocol and provider contract.
       - Preserve one in-flight request.
       - Reject stale and mismatched responses.
       - Use the same 180-second timeout.
    
    5. Safety parity
       - action_type: "OBSERVATION"
       - empty state_changes
       - zero entropy_impact
       - no schema widening
       - no runtime mutation authority
    
    6. 3D presentation
       - Feed the accepted narrative to the 3D Dragon’s HUD/log.
       - Trigger its existing response pulse.
       - Leave its flying/orbiting presentation intact.
    
    7. Later ticket
       - Add the transformation between world-scale 3D Dragon and small 2D HUD Dragon.
       - Both presentations remain attached to the same Dragon identity and session.
    
    The initial success criterion is therefore simple:
    
    text
    The human types to the 3D Dragon.
    The same persisted Dragon receives the message.
    A correlated viewport snapshot is attached.
    The Dragon describes the snapshot.
    The response is spoken/displayed through the 3D embodiment.
    A later message retains conversational context.
    
    
    No camera architecture is required for that first proof.

    The entire existing Godot project—scripts, scenes, assets, plugin, and project settings—is still untracked. I left it
    exactly that way. Before capability work, we should make a complete preservation commit of the current prototype.
    
    What the two EngAIn servers actually are
    
    1. launch_engine.py
    
    Path:
    
    /home/mytruelove/Desktop/burdens_of_a_forgotten_past/EngAIn/tier1/engainos/launch_engine.py
    
    This identifies itself as the canonical EngAIn engine bootstrap. It:
    
    - enforces the expected EngAIn root
    - checks engine invariants
    - initializes AP runtime integration
    - starts the scene/AP server
    - accepts Godot scene and AP queries
    - listens on port 8765
    
    Its surface is the scene server, not the Dragon conversation endpoint.
    
    2. sim_runtime.py
    
    Path:
    
    /home/mytruelove/Desktop/burdens_of_a_forgotten_past/EngAIn/tier2/godotsim/sim_runtime.py
    
    This is the primary simulation runtime. It:
    
    - creates EngAInRuntime
    - installs RuntimeHTTPHandler
    - owns simulation state
    - handles snapshots, scenes, commands, vaults, transforms, and embodiment contracts
    - listens on 127.0.0.1:8080
    
    Its principal routes include:
    
    text
    GET  /health
    GET  /snapshot
    GET  /transforms
    GET  /vault/status
    POST /command
    POST /scene/load
    POST /vault/link
    POST /world/sync
    POST /transforms
    POST /embodiment/apply
    
    
    It does not implement:
    
    text
    POST /v1/engain/parse
    
    
    There is also the Tier-1 FastAPI facade
    
    tier1/engainos/engainos_server.py normally fronts the runtime on port 8090.
    
    Its API includes:
    
    text
    /api/health
    /api/snapshot
    /api/command
    /api/dialogue/say
    /api/dialogue/ask
    /api/lore/uplift
    /api/hud/*
    
    
    That facade also does not implement /v1/engain/parse.
    
    What happened with port 8081
    
    I found an older quarantined copy of engainos_server.py that starts Uvicorn on port 8081:
    
    `/mnt/data-drive/burdens_of_a_forgotten_past/EngAIn/_quarantine/avatar_2026-03-07/avatar/godotengain/engainos/engainos_ser
    ver.py`
    
    That historical facade contains:
    
    text
    POST /api/lore/collaborate
    POST /api/dragon/speak
    
    
    But it still does not contain /v1/engain/parse.
    
    Its Dragon endpoint is also just a rule-based placeholder that returns:
    
    text
    EngAIn Dragon pulses with resonance: '<text>' ...
    Calculating cosmic delta.
    
    
    It was not a persisted AI brain.
    
    Historical route conclusion
    
    I searched:
    
    - the current EngAIn source
    - the recovered /mnt/data-drive EngAIn copy
    - Python and GDScript sources
    - the EngAIn Git history
    
    /v1/engain/parse appears only in EngAInBridge3D.gd clients. I found no server implementation for that route.
    
    Git history confirms that the string entered history with the old Godot clients, but not with a corresponding Python
    route.
    
    Therefore, the historical 3D bridge appears to have been an unfinished or orphaned client contract:
    
    text
    EngAInBridge3D.gd
    → expected 127.0.0.1:8081/v1/engain/parse
    → no matching preserved server implementation
    
    
    That explains why the 3D project contains a bridge-shaped client but no recoverable Dragon brain behind it.
    
    Provenance of the 3D project
    
    I compared the current 3D project with:
    
    /mnt/data-drive/burdens_of_a_forgotten_past/EngAIn/godot_engain_test/engain-parser
    
    Excluding the newly copied GodotOllama addon and new README:
    
    - Files compared: 22
    - Byte-identical: 21
    - Different: project.godot
    
    The project.godot difference is consistent with enabling the copied portable addon.
    
    So the new 3D repository is essentially the preserved early engain-parser prototype plus the portable GodotOllama tool.
    
    How the systems should be classified
    
    text
    Hermes session
    → Dragon identity, context, language, and snapshot understanding
    
    Godot 3D project
    → world/HUD embodiment and snapshot producer
    
    EngAIn sim runtime :8080
    → world state, commands, scenes, transforms, and snapshots
    
    EngAIn facade :8090
    → governed/supervised access to runtime
    
    EngAIn scene/AP server :8765
    → scene loading and AP queries
    
    GodotOllama addon
    → portable editor tool
    
    
    The EngAIn engine is real and important, but neither launch_engine.py nor sim_runtime.py should replace the proven
    Hermes Dragon-session path.
    
    For first capability parity, we do not need to revive /v1/engain/parse or wire the 3D Dragon directly into the world
    engine. We should reproduce the proven 2D Hermes mailbox, persisted session, correlated screenshot, and observation-only
    response path inside the 3D project.
    
    Later, the same project can consume EngAIn’s :8080/:8090 world surfaces through a separate, explicitly authorized
    runtime lane.
    
    Current live state

    text
    8080

    8090
    8765
    
    
    None are currently listening.
    
    Next safe action
    
    Before changing any behavior, the existing untracked 3D prototype should receive its preservation commit. That would lock:
    
    - original 3D flight behavior
    - original HTTP bridge
    - HUD
    - assets and scenes
    - exact portable GodotOllama copy
    - current project.godot
    
    After that lock, the first capability ticket can replace only the orphaned Dragon communication path while preserving its
    3D embodiment and the addon’s portability.
